Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A detector combining convolutional and attention-based neural networks, trained on synthetic geodetic time series, was applied to raw GNSS data in Cascadia from 2007 to 2022. The authors report 78 detections, including 35 of 40 events in the 2007–2017 comparison catalog and 43 additional events. Detection patterns align with tremor activity, but the paper says independent acquisition-data validation is needed, and shorter/low-confidence events remain uncertain. This method is specific to this station network and does not predict earthquakes.

6 Oct 2023École Polytechnique Fédérale de Lausanne; University of Liège; European Southern Observatory; Universidad Andrés Bello

CNN-assisted search confirms quadruply lensed quasar PS J2107−1611

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A convolutional neural network trained on simulated lens images screened 27,760 preselected quasar candidates with multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections. Researchers visually inspected the top 2,000 scores and selected follow-up targets; spectroscopy and multi-survey imaging established PS J2107−1611 as a quadruply imaged quasar at redshift 2.673, with a 4.3-arcsecond image separation and a lensed host-galaxy arc. Its fold-image optical fluxes are roughly 15 times fainter than a smooth lens-mass model predicts, while radio flux ratios are consistent with that model. The date is the original preprint posting; the journal version appeared in November 2023.

Technology: Convolutional neural network trained on simulated quadruply lensed quasars, applied to Pan-STARRS gri image cutouts

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The CNN ranked image cutouts of 27,760 quasar catalog entries that already had multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections. The team visually examined the top 2,000, selected 131 promising systems, and followed targets with imaging/spectroscopy; the model therefore enabled prioritization but did not itself confirm lensing. Human follow-up established the quasar redshift and lens configuration.
Test and result
A convolutional neural network trained on simulated lens images screened 27,760 preselected quasar candidates with multiple nearby Pan-STARRS detections.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a single confirmed system from a prefiltered candidate set; the authors visually reviewed high-scoring candidates and performed follow-up. Spectroscopy confirms the source is a quasar at z=2.673, while the four-image lens interpretation is supported by high-resolution imaging, lens modeling, a host-galaxy arc, and wavelength-dependent flux ratios. The ∼15-fold optical fold-pair anomaly is relative to the smooth macromodel; the cause is not resolved in this letter. Detailed JWST/VLT/EVN follow-up was left for future work.

26 Jul 2023CMS Collaboration; CERN

CMS makes the first hadron-collider observation of boosted Z→cc̄ decay

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, CMS observed boosted Z→cc̄ production in association with jets for the first time at a hadron collider. The measured signal strength relative to the Standard Model prediction is 1.00 with combined uncertainty +0.19/−0.17. A deep neural network (DeepDoubleX) identified large-radius jets from charm-quark pairs; the paper also sets a 95% confidence-level upper limit of 47 times the Standard Model prediction on boosted H→cc̄ production.

Technology: DeepDoubleX deep neural network for double-charm jet tagging

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The neural network processed secondary-vertex and particle-flow candidate features to classify boosted two-prong jets as charm versus light flavor and charm versus bottom. Its charm tagging separated the Z→cc̄ signal from dominant multijet backgrounds and supported the first observation in the boosted high-transverse-momentum regime.
Test and result
In 138 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton-proton data, CMS observed boosted Z→cc̄ production in association with jets for the first time at a hadron collider.
Limitation / still unknown
The first-observation claim is specifically for Z→cc̄ in association with jets at high transverse momentum at a hadron collider; it is not the first observation of Z decay generally. The neural tagger is an important analysis tool, while the observation derives from the full CMS event selection and likelihood fit. In the same paper, boosted H→cc̄ was not observed; only an upper limit is reported.

12 Jun 2023ATLAS Collaboration; CERN

ATLAS graph neural network enables first observation of four-top-quark production

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS observed simultaneous production of four top quarks in same-sign dilepton and multilepton events, with 6.1σ observed (4.3σ expected) significance. The measured cross-section is 22.5⁺⁶·⁶₋₅·₅ fb, consistent with the Standard Model prediction within 1.8σ. A graph neural network separated the four-top signal from dominant backgrounds in the signal extraction.

Technology: Event-level graph neural network built with graph_nets/TensorFlow

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The GNN discriminant classified events using a fully connected graph of reconstructed jets, leptons and missing transverse momentum, with node, edge and global features. It separated four-top signal from background and supplied the discriminant used in the signal extraction and cross-section fit.
Test and result
Using 140 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV proton–proton collisions, ATLAS observed simultaneous production of four top quarks in same-sign dilepton and multilepton events, with 6.1σ observed (4.3σ expected) significance.
Limitation / still unknown
This is an ATLAS observation from its own 140 fb⁻¹ analysis after earlier ATLAS/CMS evidence; it is one paper-level observation, not a separate count for CMS or later combinations. The measured cross-section uncertainty is large and its central value is 1.8σ above the SM prediction, so this is not evidence of a significant deviation from the Standard Model. Background control regions and systematic uncertainties are part of the analysis.

Hydrodynamic simulations of a system containing a 5-Jupiter-mass planet at 75 au reproduced the observed kinematic structure; the authors conclude that HD 142666 hosts an embedded planet by current field criteria. The planet is inferred from disk kinematics rather than directly imaged.

Test and result

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An unsupervised repeating-signal detector, combined with template matching, exposed small earthquake bursts in a region of the Eagle Ford shale where conventional catalogs had detected little seismicity. The authors report that the new events temporally coincided with hydraulic-fracturing activity, and their regional analysis found detectable seismicity began after injection flux exceeded about two million barrels per month despite similar cumulative injection volume; this supports, but does not prove, a causal role for injection rate and is limited by sparse station coverage.

14 Dec 2022Columbia University; ETH Zurich; Swiss Federal Institute for Forest, Snow and Landscape Research (WSL)

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An unsupervised analysis of five weeks of seismic records from Gornergletscher, Switzerland, separated cryoseismic signals into clusters associated with the 2007 Gornersee lake flood and with afternoon/evening meltwater flow. One high-energy, broadband noise cluster began about two days before the reported onset of subglacial flooding; the authors suggest it captures the earlier initiation of englacial and subglacial drainage and improves the flood chronology.

Technology: SpecUFEx unsupervised feature extraction: non-negative matrix factorization, hidden Markov modeling, principal component analysis, and k-means clustering

Editorial relevance 47 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The unsupervised learning pipeline reduced seismic spectrograms to fingerprints and grouped similar continuous-noise and icequake signals without labels. Comparing the learned clusters with lake level, GPS, temperature, and event timing exposed distinct clusters associated with lake-flood drainage and daily meltwater flow, which supported the revised drainage chronology.
Test and result
An unsupervised analysis of five weeks of seismic records from Gornergletscher, Switzerland, separated cryoseismic signals into clusters associated with the 2007 Gornersee lake flood and with afternoon/evening meltwater flow.
Limitation / still unknown
The analysis covers five weeks at one glacier using the 2007 flood record. The inferred drainage start precedes lake-level evidence by about two days, but the source cannot definitively exclude ice-bed slip, till deformation, or small fractures as explanations for some spectral features. The study interprets cluster timing and spectra as hydrologic signals; it does not directly image subglacial drainage pathways.

7 Nov 2022Oak Ridge National Laboratory; University of Tennessee, Knoxville

Autonomous microscopy discovers an edge plasmon in layered MnPS₃

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Deep-kernel-learning-guided STEM-EELS measurements identified a low-energy edge-localized plasmon response in the layered antiferromagnet MnPS₃, distinct from the higher-energy bulk plasmon response. The system used local structure to predict spectra and uncertainty, then autonomously selected where to measure based on a physics-defined objective. After 10–25 spectra, about 1% of the image-space points, it localized the edge mode to the flake boundary.

Technology: Deep kernel learning (feed-forward neural-network feature embedding with a Gaussian-process kernel and physics-based acquisition/scalarizer)

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The model learned a structure-to-electron-energy-loss-spectrum relationship from sequential measurements and used its predicted response and uncertainty to choose subsequent STEM probe locations. A user-specified spectral scalarizer prioritized a low-energy feature relative to the bulk plasmon; the closed-loop instrument then discovered and mapped an edge-localized response without pretraining on MnPS₃ sample data.
Test and result
Deep-kernel-learning-guided STEM-EELS measurements identified a low-energy edge-localized plasmon response in the layered antiferromagnet MnPS₃, distinct from the higher-energy bulk plasmon response.
Limitation / still unknown
The paper demonstrates the mode in the reported exfoliated MnPS₃ flake and STEM-EELS setup. The physical search objective was supplied by researchers (a spectral feature below the bulk-plasmon peak), so the AI did not independently formulate the scientific question. The authors report that the edge response varied with local boundary shape and present this as a proof-of-concept discovery workflow, not a complete theory of MnPS₃ plasmon behavior.

Finding: A ResNetV2-based semi-supervised workflow ranked images from the 20-square-degree Deep Lens Survey and surfaced two Grade-A lens systems for follow-up. Keck spectroscopy secured a source redshift of z=1.81 for DLS212072337, while DLS432021848 has only a tentative single-line redshift but supporting high-resolution Hubble morphology; the paper counts both as lensing systems, with the latter less secure. The broader visually inspected list contains additional candidates, not confirmed lenses.

Technology: ResNetV2 neural network with semi-supervised MixMatch or Pi-Model and GAN-based image augmentation

4 Aug 2022ATLAS Collaboration; CERN

ATLAS boosted decision trees enable first observation of triple-W boson production

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: In 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV LHC data, ATLAS observed simultaneous WWW production at 8.0σ (5.4σ expected) and measured an inclusive cross-section of 820 ± 100(stat) ± 80(syst) fb. The analysis selected same-sign dilepton-plus-jets and trilepton final states. Two boosted decision trees—one per channel—improved signal/background separation and supplied distributions for the fit. The measured rate is about 2.6σ above the Standard Model prediction, not a significant discrepancy.

Technology: Two boosted decision tree classifiers for the dilepton and trilepton channels

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The BDTs were trained separately for the two- and three-lepton channels, using 12 and 11 well-modelled event variables respectively, to discriminate WWW signal from background. Their output distributions entered the likelihood fit; the paper says the improved separation helped enable the observation.
Test and result
In 139 fb⁻¹ of 13 TeV LHC data, ATLAS observed simultaneous WWW production at 8.0σ (5.4σ expected) and measured an inclusive cross-section of 820 ± 100(stat) ± 80(syst) fb.
Limitation / still unknown
The final peer-reviewed paper reports 8.0σ (5.4σ expected), superseding the earlier 2021 conference-note/press account that quoted 8.2σ and 850 fb. CMS had previously observed combined inclusive production of three massive gauge bosons; this ATLAS paper is the first observation specifically of WWW production. The measured cross-section is about 2.6σ above the theory prediction, below a significant deviation, with data-driven background and systematic uncertainties included.

6 Jun 2022The Chinese University of Hong Kong; Chinese Academy of Sciences; Qingdao National Laboratory for Marine Science and Technology; Southern University of Science and Technology; Woods Hole Oceanographic Institution

EQTransformer-based catalog reveals a 50-km-deep outer-rise fault at the Southern Mariana trench

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: Applying EQTransformer phase detection to near-field ocean-bottom seismometer recordings collected near the Challenger Deep yielded more than 1,975 earthquakes. Relocation and waveform-similarity clustering outlined a southern Mariana outer-rise fault extending approximately 20–50 km below the seafloor (about 50 km maximum depth), inferred to be a reactivated normal fault; the depth suggests more incoming-plate water input than prior estimates.

Technology: EQTransformer machine-learning seismic phase detector

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Detected and picked P- and S-wave phases in near-field ocean-bottom seismometer data, producing a denser earthquake catalog. Researchers manually reviewed selected phases, relocated the events and used waveform-similarity clustering to delineate the fault.
Test and result
Applying EQTransformer phase detection to near-field ocean-bottom seismometer recordings collected near the Challenger Deep yielded more than 1,975 earthquakes.
Limitation / still unknown
The fault depth, fault type and hydration implications are inferred from a localized seismic cluster, waveform similarity, and regional tomography; focal mechanisms could not be robustly resolved, and the authors state that limited station coverage does not establish whether such deep faults are widespread.

5 Apr 2022Universidad de Valparaíso; Universidad de Chile; European Southern Observatory; Millennium Institute of Astrophysics; California Institute of Technology

Random-forest alert screening leads to spectroscopic confirmation of four changing-look AGNs

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A hierarchical random-forest classifier in the ALeRCE broker applied to Zwicky Transient Facility alert-stream light curves helped select candidate changing-look active galactic nuclei from objects previously classified as type 2. New spectra confirmed significant changes in broad Balmer emission in four of six observed candidates, establishing new type 2-to-type 1 transitions. The other two observed objects had only tentative evidence and are not counted as confirmed transitions.

Technology: ALeRCE light-curve classifier (hierarchical imbalanced random forest)

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The ALeRCE random-forest classifier used variability features from ZTF light curves and ZTF/AllWISE photometric colors to classify alert sources as AGN-like. Its classifications were cross-matched against a pre-existing spectroscopic type-2 AGN sample to produce candidates with current type-1-like variability; this AI-assisted shortlist enabled follow-up spectroscopy that confirmed the transitions.
Test and result
A hierarchical random-forest classifier in the ALeRCE broker applied to Zwicky Transient Facility alert-stream light curves helped select candidate changing-look active galactic nuclei from objects previously classified as type 2.
Limitation / still unknown
Only six of the 30 visually retained candidates received new spectra in this paper, selected partly for observability; four showed clear >3σ broad-line changes and two were tentative. Thus the reported ≥66% success rate is a small, selected follow-up sample, not an unbiased prevalence estimate. The authors note possible 30–50% misclassification in the parent type-2 sample and that the ZTF alert threshold makes the method incomplete.

20 Dec 2021Harvard University; Massachusetts Institute of Technology; Cornell University; collaborators

Machine learning reveals two previously undetected phases in Rydberg-atom quantum-simulator data

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: An interpretable hybrid unsupervised/supervised learning analysis of experimental snapshots from a programmable Rydberg-atom array mapped phase regions on a square lattice and identified signatures of two previously undetected phases: boundary-ordered and rhombic. The rhombic pattern had been predicted theoretically, so the result is its experimental-data signature rather than a wholly unanticipated theoretical phase; the boundary-ordered phase was revealed from edge correlations.

Technology: Hybrid-correlation convolutional neural network (Hybrid-CCNN) with PCA and clustering

Test and result

Limitation / still unknown
The study reanalyzes experimental quantum-simulator snapshots; the rhombic phase was previously predicted by theory, while the paper reports its finite-size experimental manifestation. The paper characterizes phases using data and numerical interpretation, not bulk material synthesis.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A deep convolutional autoencoder trained on synthetic deformation and atmospheric-noise time series recovered millimeter-scale ground motion from real InSAR without being given the fault location. Applied to COSMO-SkyMed observations of the 2013 North Anatolian Fault slow-slip event, it detected about 2 mm of slip beyond the previously identified segment and revised the estimated along-strike event length from about 10 km to 15–20 km. The same paper used the approach to resolve transient millimeter-scale subsidence at California’s Coso geothermal field. This is one paper-level finding enabled by the model’s extraction of deformation signals from noisy satellite data.

Technology: Deep convolutional autoencoder trained on synthetic InSAR time series

Editorial relevance 47 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The deep autoencoder separated persistent ground deformation patterns from atmospheric noise in real InSAR data without prior fault-location information, resolving the additional 2 mm slow-slip segment; the model was trained on synthetic data and was not fine-tuned on the real case.
Test and result
A deep convolutional autoencoder trained on synthetic deformation and atmospheric-noise time series recovered millimeter-scale ground motion from real InSAR without being given the fault location.
Limitation / still unknown
The newly resolved extension is a reanalysis of an existing 2013 InSAR event, not a newly occurring earthquake. The paper’s reported extension is model-derived; the original expert analysis had detected the larger slow-slip segment, while the autoencoder identifies an additional lower-amplitude portion. The model assumes atmospheric noise characteristics represented by its synthetic training data and omits some noise sources, including ionospheric perturbations. Coso results are a separate application within the same paper and are not counted separately.

9 Oct 2021Zwicky Transient Facility collaboration; California Institute of Technology; Liverpool John Moores University; University of California, Berkeley; collaborating observatories

Real-time discovery and characterization of AT2020xnd, an AT2018cow-like fast transient

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: ZTF observations identified AT2020xnd (ZTF20acigmel) at redshift 0.243 as the first unambiguous AT2018cow analogue found and confirmed in real time. Follow-up showed a rapid optical rise, persistently hot blue emission, luminous radio, millimetre and X-ray output, no classical radioactive-supernova peak, and a dwarf-galaxy host. The authors argue that these properties support a distinct fast-blue-optical-transient phenomenon, while its progenitor and central engine remain uncertain.

Technology: Zwicky Transient Facility real-time alert stream with a deep-learning real-bogus classifier and a custom contextual transient filter

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The paper’s custom search filter required the ZTF alert’s deep-learning real-bogus score to exceed 0.65, along with repeated detections, a high Galactic latitude and no coincident stellar counterpart. This AI-based alert filtering contributed to selecting AT2020xnd in real time; rapid multiband photometry and spectroscopic and multiwavelength human follow-up established its astrophysical nature and unusual properties.
Test and result
ZTF observations identified AT2020xnd (ZTF20acigmel) at redshift 0.243 as the first unambiguous AT2018cow analogue found and confirmed in real time.
Limitation / still unknown
This is one transient and a small class comparison, not a general population measurement. The AI contribution was alert filtering, not autonomous physical interpretation or confirmation; telescope follow-up was essential. The suggested distinct phenomenon is supported by shared observed properties, while the progenitor/engine interpretation remains unresolved and model-dependent.

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Technology: THESEUS

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution

The authors identify the work as explainable AI; the contribution is the algorithmically found design/concept, not merely faster benchmarking.
Test and result

Limitation / still unknown
These are theoretically calculated experiment blueprints, not experimentally demonstrated states in this paper. For the six-photon GHZ design, unwanted amplitudes can be reduced arbitrarily by adjusting graph weights, at the cost of lower count rates; feasibility is argued for contemporary photonics. The heralded Bell design’s estimated count rate and fidelity are modeled, not measured.

2 Jul 2021Université Grenoble Alpes; CNRS

Single-station CNN analysis adds 273 visually confirmed earthquakes to the L’Aquila near-fault catalog

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A two-stage 1D CNN pipeline applied to continuous three-component seismic waveforms from one L’Aquila station identified 273 previously uncataloged local earthquake events during a one-month 2009 foreshock period. The study reports that 84% were assigned magnitude below 1 and that all 273 new detections were visually confirmed; the expanded catalog contained roughly three times as many new detections as the pre-existing catalog had events for that period.

Technology: Two-stage one-dimensional convolutional neural network (CNN) pipeline

Editorial relevance 47 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The detector CNN scanned continuous raw three-component waveform windows for earthquake signals; a second CNN classified distance and magnitude, enabling the researchers to find and characterize uncataloged events from a single station. The CNN pipeline generated the new event detections; authors then visually checked them.
Test and result
A two-stage 1D CNN pipeline applied to continuous three-component seismic waveforms from one L’Aquila station identified 273 previously uncataloged local earthquake events during a one-month 2009 foreshock period.
Limitation / still unknown
The analysis covers one month in February 2009 from one station. The authors report 273 new local detections and six nonlocal events among 369 windows classified as local; all 273 new detections were visually confirmed. The pipeline does not provide exact event locations, and transfer to other regions was not established. Magnitudes are coarse classes, not precise estimates.

6 Dec 2020Princeton University; DeepMind; New York University; Flatiron Institute; Carnegie Mellon University

AI-derived symbolic formula improves dark-matter halo overdensity estimates in a cosmological simulation

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A graph neural network trained on a detailed N-body cosmological dark-matter simulation was distilled by symbolic regression into an analytic estimator for a halo’s overdensity from its mass and the masses, positions and velocities of nearby halos. On the analyzed simulation snapshot, the new expression reduced mean absolute error to 0.0882, compared with 0.121 for the authors’ existing hand-designed estimator. The formula’s mass-weighted neighborhood term decreases with separation and includes dependence on the central halo’s mass.

Technology: Sparse-representation Graph Neural Network followed by Eureqa genetic-programming symbolic regression

Editorial relevance 40 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The team trained a graph neural network to predict dark-matter halo overdensity using a cosmological N-body simulation. L1 regularization encouraged a compact learned message representation; Eureqa symbolic regression then fitted explicit equations to the learned graph-network components and replaced them with a closed-form model. This process yielded a previously unreported analytic halo-environment estimator and enabled comparison against the existing hand-designed formula.
Test and result
A graph neural network trained on a detailed N-body cosmological dark-matter simulation was distilled by symbolic regression into an analytic estimator for a halo’s overdensity from its mass and the masses, positions and velocities of nearby halos.
Limitation / still unknown
The formula was derived and evaluated on a single final-time-step dataset from an N-body simulation (215,854 halos), not validated against observations or independent cosmological simulations. The authors note that some terms’ physical interpretation was unclear and reserved detailed interpretation for future astrophysics work. It is a new useful estimator for the studied simulated halo population, not a confirmed universal law of dark-matter structure.

24 Aug 2020University of Warwick

Machine-learning validation and independent cross-checks classify 50 Kepler candidates as planets

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A Gaussian-process classifier, supported by random-forest, extra-trees and multilayer-perceptron models, was applied to Kepler transit candidates. From 83 candidates meeting the study’s stringent ML and data-quality criteria, the authors newly validated 50 as planets after updated vespa false-positive checks also gave each less than a 1% false-positive probability. The result is statistical validation using Kepler pipeline and stellar data, not new telescope observations of the planets.

Technology: Gaussian process classifier, random forest, extra trees and multilayer perceptron classifiers

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Four machine-learning classifiers jointly ranked candidate transit signals against planet and false-positive scenarios. The paper required all four to cross 0.99 planet probability plus positional, stellar, binary and outlier checks; an updated vespa calculation then independently cross-checked the retained candidates.
Test and result
A Gaussian-process classifier, supported by random-forest, extra-trees and multilayer-perceptron models, was applied to Kepler transit candidates.
Limitation / still unknown
The 50 are statistically validated candidates from archival Kepler observations, not newly observed planets. The authors caution that their method is trained and demonstrated on Kepler, that atypical/outlier candidates may be unreliable, and that ML and vespa disagree for 33 other high-ML candidates; those were not counted as validated. 16 Jul 2020University of Auckland; University of Otago

Machine learning identifies seismic tremor precursors to Whakaari eruptions

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A structured machine-learning analysis of nine years of Whakaari seismic records identified four-hour energy bursts that occurred hours to days before most of the five major eruptive episodes studied. The authors interpret the pattern as possible charging of the vent hydrothermal system by hot magmatic fluids. In cross-validation, the prototype alert model gave advance warning for four of five unseen eruption instances, including at least four hours before the 2019 eruption.

Technology: Structured machine-learning pipeline using engineered seismic time-series features, a random forest, and an alert decision model

Editorial relevance 47 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
The machine-learning pipeline analyzed continuous seismic tremor data, selected precursor patterns, and modeled short-term eruption alerts. Its feature analysis identified the four-hour energy-burst pattern; the proposed hydrothermal charging mechanism is the authors’ geophysical interpretation, not a direct measurement of subsurface fluid movement.
Test and result
A structured machine-learning analysis of nine years of Whakaari seismic records identified four-hour energy bursts that occurred hours to days before most of the five major eruptive episodes studied.
Limitation / still unknown
This is a study of five major eruptive episodes at one volcano. The 4/5 warning result is cross-validation on this historical record, not independent operational validation; the model was described as a prototype. The ensemble alert score is not a calibrated eruption probability, and alert thresholds trade missed events against false alarms. The proposed hydrothermal charging mechanism is an interpretation of the seismic pattern.

29 Apr 2020SLAC National Accelerator Laboratory

LCLS magnet tuning with machine learning

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: A physics-informed Gaussian process using historical data tunes the accelerator’s 24 LCLS quadrupole magnets. The authors estimate tuning is about three to five times faster, with beams tending to be more intense; this is a result on the real machine, limited to this part of the tuning process.

Technology: Gaussian-process optimization

Editorial relevance 60 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Peer-reviewed paper · Test context: direct test. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Gaussian process model predicted the effect of magnet adjustments, chose which to try, and was trained on past machine data.
Test and result
Applied to the LCLS X-ray free-electron laser; the abstract reports the Bayesian optimizer as sample-efficient and outperforming existing optimizers, and the press release estimates tuning three to five times faster.
Limitation / still unknown
Speed and beam-quality gains are estimates; the method covers only part of the tuning process; results from three other accelerators are not reported.

6 Apr 2020DeepMind

Graph networks to understand glass dynamics

Source check: Source checked. Not independent scientific confirmation.

Finding: The networks predict particle mobility in simulated glasses; perturbing the network reveals growing spatial correlations as the glass transition is approached. Evidence comes from simulations: the learned correlation length does not translate directly into a measurable physical quantity.

Technology: Graph neural networks

Editorial relevance 53 physics Original sourcePaper Suggest a correction

Publication: Publication status unspecified in annotation · Test context: retrospective or benchmark. Catalog categories, not an evidence grade.

AI contribution
Graph neural networks predicted particle mobility and probed spatial correlations by perturbing particles in shells around a marked particle.
Test and result
Networks trained on simulated glasses predicted particle mobility and were checked against simulation ground truth, outperforming physics-inspired and state-of-the-art baselines (96% correlation at short timescales, 64% at relaxation time).
Limitation / still unknown
The network's correlation length cannot be directly converted into a measurable physical quantity; it remains an open debate; the work models simulated glasses, not physical samples.
Evidence sources and annotation context

Evidence context (descriptions follow the cited sources; test type is a catalog classification):

Evidence sources: https://deepmind.google/blog/towards-understanding-glasses-with-graph-neural-networks/
